applied in the
\xi_{1}
variable implies

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi iN\xi_{1}x_{1}-it4\pi^{2}N^{2}\xi_{1}^{2}}\chi_{1}^{2}(\xi_{1},\eta^{\prime})\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\xi_{1}\Big|\lesssim(1+|N^{2}t|+|Nx_{1}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq(1+|Nx_{1}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

with an implicit constant independent of
\eta^{\prime}
. Hence, (
2.25
) follows, completing the proof of
(
2.4
).
∎

Proof of
Lemma

2.5
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
l=1
. Bounds (
2.5
) and (
2.5
) for
\mathfrak{c}\geq 2
follow from (
2.5
) for
\mathfrak{c}=2
.
Indeed, for any fixed
(t,x_{1})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
from Bernstein’s inequality (
Lemma

2.3
and see the proof of Lemma
2.4
for details) in the
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
variable we obtain

\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{1}}f(x_{1},\cdot)\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\lesssim N^{(d-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{c}})}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{1}}f(x_{1},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}

and if
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{f}))\leq 2R
we obtain

\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{1}}f(x_{1},\cdot)\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\lesssim R^{(d-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{c}})}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{1}}f(x_{1},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\,.

We prove (
2.5
) for
\mathfrak{c}=2
by showing that

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}}\|T_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{1}}f(\cdot,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{1}}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where
T_{N}
is defined in (
2
). Plancherel’s identity in
x^{\prime}
and (
2
) imply for any
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
that

\displaystyle\|T_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{1}}f(\cdot,x_{1},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathclap{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}}\;\,\bigg|\!\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\xi_{1}x_{1}-4\pi^{2}it(|\xi_{1}|^{2}+|\xi^{\prime}|^{2})}\chi_{N}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})d\xi_{1}\bigg|^{2}\!\!\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\xi^{\prime}.

Note that if we prove the fiber-wise bound

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\xi_{1}x_{1}-4\pi^{2}it|\xi_{1}|^{2}}\chi_{N}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\xi_{1}\bigg|^{2}\!\!\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\chi_{N}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})|^{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\xi_{1}

for any fixed
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
, then (
2.5
) with
\mathfrak{c}=2
follows after integration in
\xi^{\prime}
and an application of Plancherel’s identity. Split the integration domain in
\xi_{1}
as
\mathbb{R}=(0,\infty)\cup(-\infty,0)
and we only show (
2
) for
(0,\infty)
in place of
\mathbb{R}
. The case of
(-\infty,0)
is treated similarly. A change of variables
\theta=\xi_{1}^{2}
and Plancherel’s identity in
t
imply that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{i2\pi\xi_{1}x_{1}-4\pi^{2}it|\xi_{1}|^{2}}\chi_{N}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\xi_{1}\bigg|^{2}\!\!\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi ix_{1}\sqrt{\theta}-4\pi^{2}it\theta}\chi_{N}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\chi_{\theta\geq 0}\frac{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\theta}{\sqrt{4\theta}}\bigg|^{2}\!\!\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big|e^{2\pi ix_{1}\sqrt{\theta}}\chi_{N}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\chi_{\theta\geq 0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\theta}}\Big|^{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big|e^{2\pi ix_{1}\sqrt{\theta}}\chi_{N}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\Big|^{2}\frac{1}{4\theta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\theta\,.

Finally, the inverse change of variables
\xi_{1}=\sqrt{\theta}
gives that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big|e^{2\pi ix_{1}\sqrt{\theta}}\chi_{N}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\sqrt{\theta},\xi^{\prime})\Big|^{2}\frac{1}{4\theta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big|e^{2\pi ix_{1}\xi_{1}}\chi_{N}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\Big|^{2}\frac{1}{\xi_{1}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}\xi_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\chi_{N}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\widehat{f}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})|^{2}d\xi_{1},

where the last inequality holds because
\chi_{N}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{U}_{e_{1}}}(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})=0
unless

|(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})|\approx N\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{|\xi_{1}|}{|(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})|}>(\sqrt{4d})^{-1}\,,

which implies that
\frac{1}{\xi_{1}}\lesssim N^{-1}
. The required bound (
2
) follows.
∎

3.
Non-homogeneous estimates

In
Section

2
we established estimates on
solutions of the linear homogeneous Cauchy problem
(
2
). In this section we study solutions
to the non-homogeneous Schrödinger equation

\begin{cases}(i\partial_{t}+\Delta)v=h(t,x)&\text{on }\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
v(0,x)=v_{0}(x)\,.&\end{cases}

We begin by introducing two norms,
X^{\mathfrak{s}}
and
Y^{S}
, that are
obtained as combinations of norms introduced in

Section

2
. Next, we obtain boundedness
properties of the solutions to (
3
)
in terms of
X^{\mathfrak{s}}
and
Y^{S}
. The solution
v
of
(
3
) can be expressed using
Duhamel’s formula (
1
) as

v=\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)\coloneqq e^{it\Delta}v_{0}-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}h(s,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\,.

Therefore our results can be seen as properties of
the mapping
(v_{0},h)\mapsto\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)=v
.

For any time interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
,
I\ni 0
we introduce two families of
space-time norms denoted
X^{\mathfrak{s}}(I)
and
Y^{S}(I)
. The norms

X^{\mathfrak{s}}(I)
are well suited to control the solution
v
with no
restriction on the Fourier support, whereas
Y^{S}(I)
is adapted to
controlling the solution
v
when

\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{v_{0}})\big)\lesssim 1
and

\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{h})\big)\lesssim 1
. Later, in

Section

6
, we show that, thanks
to the unit scale randomization of the initial data, the terms in the
explicit multilinear expansion of the solution
(
1
) can also be controlled in the

Y^{S}(I)
norms.

To be more precise, let
\varepsilon_{0}\in\big(0,2^{-100}\big)
be sufficiently small
(to be chosen appropriately below depending on other parameters) and
we allow all subsequent constants to depend implicitly on

\varepsilon_{0}
. For any interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
and for any
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
we set

\displaystyle\|v\|_{X^{\sigma}(I)}\coloneqq\Big(\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{2\sigma}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v\|^{2}_{X_{N}(I)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|v\|_{X_{N}(I)}\coloneqq\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}\\
&\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\Big(N^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\Big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\Big(N^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}+N^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\Big)\,,\end{aligned}

and

\displaystyle\|v\|_{Y^{\sigma}(I)}\coloneqq\Big(\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{2\sigma}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v\|^{2}_{Y_{N}(I)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|v\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\coloneqq\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}\\
&\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\Big(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\Big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\Big(N^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}+N^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\Big).\end{aligned}

Recall that
\mathfrak{c}_{0}\coloneqq 2\frac{d-1}{d-2}
is the lower bound for the spatial
integrability exponent that appears in
Lemma

2.4
.

The norms
X_{N}
and
Y_{N}
consist of a combination of different
space-time norms. In particular, the norms

L_{t}^{\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}
and

L_{t}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}
are close
to the admissible Strichartz norms
L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}
and

L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}
respectively (cfr.

Lemma

2.1
). The other components appearing in the
definitions of the norms
X_{N}
and
Y_{N}
are directional
space-time norms, close to the directional maximal norms of

Lemma

2.4
and to the directional smoothing norms of

Lemma

2.5
. Note that the norms appearing in the
definitions of
X_{N}
and
Y_{N}
are the same, and they differ by
the
N
-dependent scaling factors that appears next to each
summand. These scaling factors are determined by bounds
(
2.4
), (
2.5
) or by bounds
(
2.4
) and
(
2.5
) respectively.

To control the non-homogeneous term
h
we use the norms

X^{*,\sigma}(I)
, related through duality to the space

X^{\sigma}(I)
. Specifically, we define

\displaystyle\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma}(I)}\coloneqq\big(\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{2\sigma}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\|^{2}_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|h\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\coloneqq\sup\Big\{\Big|\int_{I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}h_{*}(t,x)h(t,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|\colon\|h_{*}\|_{X_{N}(I)}\leq 1\Big\}

We record the following simple facts about all the norms introduced above.

•

The norms
X^{\sigma}
,
X^{*,\sigma}
, and
Y^{\sigma}
are non-decreasing with respect to
\sigma
.

•

Since
N\geq 1
, for any
v\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
we have

\|v\|_{X_{N}(I)}\leq\|v\|_{Y_{N}(I)},\quad\text{and}\quad\|v\|_{X^{\sigma}(I)}\leq\|v\|_{Y^{\sigma}(I)}\quad\forall\sigma\in\mathbb{R}.

•

For any
N,N^{\prime}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
with
N^{\prime}\approx N
it holds that

\|v\|_{X_{N}(I)}\approx\|v\|_{X_{N^{\prime}}(I)},\qquad\|v\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\approx\|v\|_{Y_{N^{\prime}}(I)}\,,

and in particular, the definition (
2
) implies that

\big\|\widetilde{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}}_{N}v\big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\approx\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}N^{\prime}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}\\
\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}<\nicefrac{{N^{\prime}}}{{N}}<8\end{subarray}}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N^{\prime}}v\|_{X_{N^{\prime}}(I)}\,.

•

Since
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}
are convolution operators with

L^{1}
-bounded kernels, Young’s convolution inequality yields
that

\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v\big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\lesssim\big\|v\big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\,.

•

For any
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
, the space
C^{\infty}_{c}(I\times\mathbb{R})
is dense in the spaces
X^{\sigma}(I)
and
Y^{\sigma}(I)
since all exponents appearing in the definition of the corresponding norms are finite.

•

The space
X_{N}^{*}(I)
has been introduced as the dual space
of
X_{N}(I)
; it can be shown that

\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma}(I)}\approx\sup\Big\{\Big|\int_{I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}h^{*}(t,x)h(t,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|\colon\big\|h^{*}\big\|_{X^{-\sigma}(I)}\leq 1\Big\}\,,

where the supremum is taken over

h^{*}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. However, we never use this fact in this
paper.

Next, we formulate the main result of this section.

Proposition 3.1
.

Fix
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
,
0<\varepsilon\lesssim 1
, and
0<\varepsilon_{0}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}1
. Then there exists a constant

c=c(\sigma,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0})>0
such that for any
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
,
I\ni 0
,
it holds that

\big\|\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)\big\|_{X^{\sigma}(I)}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\Big(\|v_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma+\varepsilon}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(I)}\Big)\,.

If
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{v_{0}})\big)\leq 2R
and
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{h})\big)\leq 2R
, then

\big\|\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)\big\|_{Y^{\sigma}(I)}\lesssim_{R,\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\Big(\|v_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma+\varepsilon}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(I)}\Big)\,.

More specifically, for any
N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
, we have

\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)\big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\big(\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\big)

and, if
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{v_{0}})\big)\leq 2R
, and
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{h})\big)\leq 2R
, then

\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)\big\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\lesssim_{R,\varepsilon}N^{\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\big(\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\big)\,.

All implicit constants are allowed to depend on
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon_{0}
, but are independent of
\sigma
,
I
,
N
, and
v_{0}
and
h
.

Remark 3.2
.

Notice that the function
|I|\mapsto\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}
is increasing and
satisfies

\displaystyle\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\lesssim_{c}|I|^{c},\qquad\lim_{|I|\to 0}\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}=0,\qquad\lim_{|I|\to\infty}\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}=1.

Next, we state
Proposition

3.3
and

Proposition

3.4
. We then provide the proofs of these
statements before focusing on the proof of

Proposition

3.1
.

Proposition

3.3
is an
analogue of
Proposition

3.1
except with

\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)
controlled by Sobolev norms. This statement is used
to bound solutions to (
1
) in more
classical spaces.
Proposition

3.4
is the adjoint bound to

Proposition

3.1
with
h=0
. This estimate is central
to prove scattering results.

Proposition 3.3
.

Under the assumptions of
Proposition

3.1
it holds that

\big\|\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)\big\|_{C^{0}(I,H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\|v_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(I)}.

Proposition 3.4
.

Under the assumptions of
Proposition

3.1
, for any
function
h\in X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})
the limit

\lim_{t\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s

exists in
H_{x}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies

\Big\|\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H_{x}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}.

Furthermore, if
v_{0}\in H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
then the limit

\lim_{t\to+\infty}e^{-it\Delta}\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)(t)

exists in
H_{x}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies

\big\|e^{-it\Delta}\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)(t)\big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\|v_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(I)}.

Proof of
Proposition

3.3
.

For conciseness, let
v\coloneqq\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)
. To show (
3.3
) it is sufficient to prove the point-wise bound

\|v(t)\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(I)}.

for any
t\in I
. Indeed, (
3
) implies that

\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(I,H^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(I)}\,,

and continuity of
t\mapsto\big\|\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)\big\|(t)
is trivial for the dense class of
functions
v_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The
claim (
3.3
) then follows by approximation.

Fix
t\in I
and let us deduce (
3
) by duality from
Proposition

3.1
. By Duhamel’s formula (
1
) we have

\big\|v(t)\big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\big\|e^{it\Delta}v_{0}\big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The definition of the Sobolev spaces (
•
‣
1.4
) and of the Schrödinger evolution group (
2
), imply for any
t\in I
that

\|e^{it\Delta}v_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|v_{0}\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and

\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Then, using the
H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
–
H^{-\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
duality and that
\sum_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\widetilde{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}
is the identity operator (see (
2
)), we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{v_{*}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\overline{v_{*}(x)}e^{-is\Delta}h(s,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{v_{*}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\overline{e^{is\Delta}v_{*}(x)}h(s,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{v_{*}}\Big|\sum_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\int_{0}^{t}\overline{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}e^{is\Delta}v_{*}(x)}\widetilde{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}}_{N}h(s,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|\,,

where the supremum is taken over
v_{*}\in H^{-\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\|v_{*}\|_{H^{-\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 1
. Using the triangle inequality, the
X_{N}(I)
–
X_{N}^{*}(I)
duality, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, as well as (
•
‣
3
) with the definition (
3
) of the
X_{N}
norm, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v_{*}}\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{-\sigma-\varepsilon}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}e^{it\Delta}v_{*}\big\|_{X_{N}(I)}N^{\sigma+\varepsilon}\big\|\widetilde{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v_{*}}\big\|e^{it\Delta}v_{*}\big\|_{X^{-\sigma-\varepsilon}(I)}\big\|h\big\|_{X^{\sigma+\varepsilon,*}(I)}\,,

Then (
3.1
) with
\sigma
replaced by
-\sigma-\varepsilon
, together with the condition
\|v_{*}\|_{H^{-\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 1
provides us with the bound

\big\|e^{it\Delta}v_{*}(t,x)\big\|_{X^{-\sigma-\varepsilon}(I)}\lesssim\|v_{*}\|_{H^{-\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 1,

and, therefore,

\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\big\|h\big\|_{X^{\sigma+\varepsilon,*}(I)}\,,

as desired.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.4
.

Since
\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
and all norms in
X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})
are Lebesgue norms with finite integrability exponent, it holds that

\lim_{T\to\infty}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{[T,\infty)}h\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}=0\,.

We claim that
\Big(t\mapsto\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big)_{t>0}
is Cauchy in
H^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
t\to+\infty
. Indeed, by (
3
)
for any
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
there exists
T_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}>0
such that

\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{[T_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}},\infty)}h\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\widetilde{\varepsilon}\,.

Then, for any
t_{1},t_{2}
with
T_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}
one has, by (
3.3
),

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\,\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s-\int_{0}^{t_{1}}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\,\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H_{x}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-is\Delta}\mathbbm{1}_{[t_{1},t_{2})}(s)h(s)\,\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H_{x}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{[t_{1},t_{2}]}h\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{[T_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}},\infty)}h\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\widetilde{\varepsilon}\,.

Hence,

\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\qquad\text{ exists in }H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Finally, using (
3.3
) once more

\Big\|\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H_{x}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-is\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|h\|_{X^{*,\sigma+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}

and (
3.4
) follows.

The existence of the limit (
3.4
) and the bound (
3.4
) follow from (
3.4
) and from the bound (
3.4
) by noticing that

e^{-it\Delta}\mathbb{I}_{v_{0}}(h)(t)=v_{0}\mp\int_{0}^{t}e^{-s\Delta}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s.

∎

The proof of
Proposition

3.1
relies on two key
steps. First, in
Lemma

3.5
below, we
prove estimates analogous to (
3.1
) and
(
3.1
) with (see Duhamel’s
formula (
1
))

h\mapsto v=\int_{I}\mathbbm{1}_{s<t}\,e^{i(t-s)\Delta}h(s,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s

replaced by

h\mapsto\int_{I}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}h(s,x)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\,.

Second, the “Christ-Kiselev” procedure illustrated in the proof of

Proposition

3.1
allows one to deduce bounds on (
3
) from the bounds on (
3
), completing the proof.

Lemma 3.5
.

Fix any

0<\varepsilon\lesssim 1
, any
0<\varepsilon_{0}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}1
. There exists a constant

c=c(\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0})>0
such that for any
I\subset\mathbb{R}
and any
N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
one has

\Big\|\int_{I}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}N^{\varepsilon}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\,.

If in addition
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{h})\big)\leq 2R
, then

\Big\|\int_{I}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\lesssim_{R,\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}N^{\varepsilon}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}.

All implicit constants are allowed to depend on
\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}
but are independent of
I
,
N
, and
h
.

Proof of
Lemma

3.5
.

For this proof we allow implicit constants to depend without mention on
\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}
, and
R>0
(if the support is assumed to be bounded).

Step 1
We claim that for any
g\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we have

\Big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\Big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and if
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{g})\big)\leq 2R
, then

\Big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\Big\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Indeed, using Bernstein’s inequality (see
Lemma

2.3
) and Strichartz’s estimate (see
Lemma

2.1
)
for admissible pairs
(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-2\varepsilon_{0}/d})
and
(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{d-2+2\varepsilon_{0}})

we find

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}}_{t}L^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}_{x}(I)}\begin{aligned} &\lesssim N^{|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}}_{t}L^{\frac{2}{1-2\varepsilon_{0}/d}}_{x}(I)}\\
&\lesssim N^{|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}\begin{aligned} &\lesssim N^{|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2+2\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}\\
&\lesssim N^{|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{aligned}

Using interpolation between
L_{e_{l}}^{(2,\infty,\infty)}(I)
and
L_{e_{l}}^{(\infty,2,2)}(I)
norms, the directional maximal estimate (
2.4
) with
\mathfrak{c}=\infty
, the directional smoothing estimate (
2.5
) with
\mathfrak{c}=2
, and the boundedness of
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\lesssim\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(2,\infty,\infty)}(I)}^{1-|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\infty,2,2)}(I)}^{|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N^{\frac{d-1}{2}+|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim N^{\frac{d-1}{2}+|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\lesssim\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(2,\infty,\infty)}(I)}^{|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\infty,2,2)}(I)}^{1-|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}+|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}+|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Also, an interpolation between
L_{e_{l}}^{(2,\infty,\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)
and
L_{e_{l}}^{(\infty,2,\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)
norms, directional estimates (
2.4
) and (
2.5
) with
\mathfrak{c}=\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}>\mathfrak{c}_{0}
, and the boundedness
of
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
yield

\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}+|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Finally, an interpolation between
L_{e_{l}}^{(\infty,2,\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)
and
L_{e_{l}}^{(2,\infty,\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)
norms and directional estimates (
2.4
) and (
2.5
) with
\mathfrak{c}=\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}
and small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
yield

\big\|e^{is\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}g\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}\lesssim N^{\frac{d-2}{2}+|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Summing these estimates provides us with (
3
)
as long as
\varepsilon_{0}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}1
.

If
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{g})\big)\leq 2R
, then
the proof of (
3
) is analogous to the proof of
(
3
), where we respectively use
(
2.4
) and (
2.5
) instead of (
2.4
) and (
2.5
).

Step 2
We claim that if
|I|<1
, then (independently of the support of
\widehat{g}
):

\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{X_{N}(I)}+\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\lesssim N^{d}|I|^{\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Note that every summand in the definitions (
3
) and (
3
) of
X_{N}(I)
and
Y_{N}(I)
is a norm of the form
L^{p}_{t}L^{q}_{x}(I)
with
p,q\in\big[2,\nicefrac{{2}}{{\varepsilon_{0}}}\big]
, or
L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{l}}(I)
for some
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c}\in\big[2,\nicefrac{{2}}{{\varepsilon_{0}}}\big]
, and
l\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
. Then the Strichartz estimates (
Lemma

2.1
), Bernstein’s inequality (
Lemma

2.3
), and Hölder’s inequality, yield

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{p}_{t}L^{q}_{x}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\frac{d}{2}}\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{p}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{d}|I|^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}\lesssim N^{d}|I|^{\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

and if
\mathfrak{a}\geq\mathfrak{b}
, then (after an exchange of integrals with respect to
x_{1}
and
t
)

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{l}}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\frac{d}{2}}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{d}|I|^{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{b}}}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(I)}\lesssim N^{d}|I|^{\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

If
\mathfrak{a}<\mathfrak{b}
, then
\mathfrak{c}>\mathfrak{c}_{0}
and the directional maximal estimate (
2.4
) implies that

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{l}}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{(2,\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{l}}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}|I|^{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{b}}}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{(2,\infty,\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{l}}(I)}\lesssim N^{d}|I|^{\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Thus, (
3
) follows from Minkowski’s inequality.

Step 3

Interpolating bounds (
3
) with (
3
) gives for
\theta=\frac{\varepsilon}{d}\in(0,1)
that

\displaystyle\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{X_{N}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\Big(N^{\theta d+(1-\theta)\varepsilon}|I|^{\theta\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}},N^{\varepsilon}\Big)\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{2\varepsilon}\langle|I|^{-1}\rangle^{-c}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

for
c=c_{\theta}=\frac{\varepsilon\varepsilon_{0}}{2d}
. Analogously, if
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{g})\big)\leq 2R
,
by interpolating (
3
) with (
3
)
one obtains

\big\|e^{it\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}g\big\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\lesssim N^{2\varepsilon}\langle|I|^{-1}\rangle^{-c}\|g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Step 4

The rest of the proof closely follows
[
CFU22
, Lemma
3.2
]
, but for completeness we recall details here. By dualizing (
3
) we obtain that

\Big\|\int_{I}e^{-is\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}

for any
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Consequently, (
3
) applied to the function
g:=\int_{I}e^{-is\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s
implies

\Big\|\int_{I}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\begin{aligned} &=\Big\|e^{it\Delta}\int_{I}e^{-is\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\\
&\lesssim N^{2\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\Big\|\int_{I}e^{-is\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\lesssim N^{3\varepsilon}\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\end{aligned}

and (
3.5
) follows
with
\varepsilon
replaced by
3\varepsilon
.
Similarly, if
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{h})\big)\leq 2R
, then from (
3
) and (
3
) it follows that

\Big\|\int_{I}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\begin{aligned} &=\Big\|e^{it\Delta}\int_{I}e^{-is\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{Y_{N}(I)}\\
&\lesssim\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}N^{2\varepsilon}\Big\|\int_{I}e^{-is\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\lesssim\big\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{c}N^{3\varepsilon}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}\,,\end{aligned}

where we used that if
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{h})\big)\leq 2R
, then
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{diam}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}(\widehat{g})\big)\leq 2R
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Next, we finish the proof of
Proposition

3.1
by
employing the “Christ-Kiselev” procedure: a dyadic partitioning
argument of the time direction. In its simplest form the
“Christ-Kiselev” Lemma can be stated as follows. Suppose that

T:L^{p}(\mathbb{R})\to L^{q}(\mathbb{R})
is a bounded linear operator, and

1\leq p<q\leq\infty
, then the maximal operator

T_{*}:L^{p}(\mathbb{R})\to L^{q}(\mathbb{R})
defined as

T_{*}h(t)\coloneqq\sup_{c}\Big|T\big(\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,c)}h\big)(t)\Big|.

is also bounded from
L^{p}(\mathbb{R})
to
L^{q}(\mathbb{R})
.

In our case the operator (
3
) plays the role of

T
, while (
3
) is point-wise controlled by
T_{*}
. The
added difficulty stems from the fact that our norms (
X_{N}
,

Y_{N}
, and
X_{N}^{*}
) are not simple Lebesgue norms in the time
variable. However, we use that all integrability exponents in the
space-time norm
X_{N}
and
Y_{N}
are larger than
2
, while the
norm
X_{N}^{*}
can be thought of as a combination of mixed-exponent
Lebesgue space with exponents smaller than
2
. Since we employ a
procedure similar to our previous work
[
CFU22
]
, we merely sketch the
proof, and we highlight the differences due to the different
definitions of the spaces
X_{N}(I)
and
Y_{N}(I)
.

Proof of
Proposition

3.1
.

Since the proof follows the arguments of
[
CFU22
, Lemma 3.3]
, we only
provide main ideas and highlight differences, which are due to the
specific definitions of the spaces
X_{N}(I)
and
Y_{N}(I)
. We allow
implicit constants to depend on
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon_{0}
without mention.

The estimate (
3.1
) follows from
(
3.1
) after squaring, multiplying by

N^{2\sigma}
, and summing with respect to
N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
. Thus, we concentrate
on (
3.1
). Also, we only focus on the proof
of (
3.1
) since the proof of
(
3.1
) only differ in the
definition of the space
\widetilde{X}_{N}
below, with obvious modifications.

The estimate (
3.1
) follows from the
Duhamel’s formula (
1
), and from the linear homogeneous
bound (
3
) once we prove

\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{X_{N}(I)}\lesssim N^{|O(\varepsilon_{0})|}\langle|I|^{-1}\big\rangle^{-c}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}.

To prove (
3
), let us define the norm

\|v\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\coloneqq\begin{aligned} &\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}L_{x}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\\
&\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\Big(N^{-\frac{d-1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\big\|v\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}+N^{-\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\big\|v\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\Big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\Big(\begin{aligned} &N^{\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\|U_{e_{l}}v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\\
&\qquad+N^{-\frac{d-2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\|U_{e_{l}}v\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\Big),\end{aligned}\end{aligned}

that is equivalent to
X_{N}(I)
uniformly in
N
and in the interval
I
. We claim that the norm
\widetilde{X}_{N}(I)
possesses the following property:

\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\leq\|v_{1}\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(J_{1})}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}+\|v_{2}\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(J_{2})}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}

as long as
v_{1},v_{2}\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
are supported respectively on disjoint time intervals
J_{1},J_{2}\subset I
.
Let us postpone showing (
3
) until the end of the proof of the proposition.

By assuming (
3
), the rest of the proof follows
the proof of
[
CFU22
, Lemma 3.3]
line by line , and therefore we only
outline main ideas. The norm
\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(I)
, dual to
\widetilde{X}_{N}(I)

(cf. (
3
)), possesses a property converse to (
3
): for
any functions
h_{1},h_{2}\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
supported on disjoint time
intervals
J_{1},J_{2}\subset I
it holds that

\big\|h_{1}+h_{2}\big\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}\geq\|h_{1}\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(J_{1})}^{\frac{2}{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}+\|h_{2}\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(J_{2})}^{\frac{2}{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}\,.

Consequently, by induction, if functions
(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are supported
on disjoint time intervals
(J_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, then

(3.35)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k}v_{k}\Big\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\leq\sum_{k}\|v_{k}\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(J_{k})}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\,.

Analogously, if
(h_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are supported on disjoint time
intervals
(J_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, then

(3.36)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k}h_{k}\Big\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(I)}^{\frac{2}{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}\geq\sum_{k}\|h_{k}\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(J_{k})}^{\frac{2}{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}\,.

Fix
h\colon I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
and without loss of generality suppose
I=[0,T_{0}]
. Then, there is a sequence of intervals
\big\{I^{n}_{k}=[t^{n}_{k},t^{n}_{k+1})\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N},k\in\{0,\ldots,2^{n}-1\}}
such
that
t_{k}^{n}\leq t^{n}_{k+1}
, the intervals
\{I^{n}_{k}\}_{k\in\{0,\ldots,2^{n}-1\}}
form a partition of
I
and
I^{n}_{k}=I_{2k}^{n+1}\cup I_{2k+1}^{n+1}
. Furthermore, the intervals are
constructed so that

\|h\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(I^{n}_{k})}\lesssim 2^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon_{0}}{2}n}\|h\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(I)}.

The construction of such intervals is provided in
[
CFU22
, Lemma 3.3]

and uses only the continuity of the map

s\mapsto\|\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,s)}(t)h(t,x)\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}}.

Then for any
t\in I
we have

\mathbbm{1}_{[0,t)}(s)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sum^{2^{n}-1}_{k=0}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{k}^{n}}(s)\mathbbm{1}_{I_{k+1}^{n}}(t)

and by triangle inequality and (
3.35
) for any regular

(t,s,x)\mapsto F(t,s,x)

\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}F(t,s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(I)}\lesssim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big({\sum^{2^{n}-1}_{k=0}}\Big\|\int_{I_{k}^{n}}F(t,s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(I_{k+1}^{n})}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\Big)^{\frac{1-\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}\,,

where we suppressed the dependence of
F
on
x
. For more details see

[
CFU22
, proof of Lemma 3.3]
. Then, since norms
X_{N}(I)
and

\widetilde{X}_{N}(I)
are equivalent and
I_{k}^{n}
and
I_{k+1}^{n}

are disjoint, it follows from (
3.36
), from

Lemma

3.5
with
F(t,s)=e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)
, and from (
3
) that

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{X_{N}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big({\sum^{2^{n}-1}_{k=0}}\Big\|\int_{I_{k}^{n}}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h(s)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}s\Big\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}(I_{k+1}^{n})}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\Big)^{\frac{1-\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}\langle|I|^{-1}\rangle^{-c}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum^{2^{n}-1}_{k=0}\|h\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(I_{k}^{n})}^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\Big)^{\frac{1-\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}\langle|I|^{-1}\rangle^{-c}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum^{2^{n}-1}_{k=0}({2^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon_{0}}{2}n}})^{\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\Big)^{\frac{1-\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{\widetilde{X}_{N}^{*}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}\langle|I|^{-1}\rangle^{-c}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\big({2^{n}}2^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}n}\big)^{\frac{1-\varepsilon_{0}}{2}}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}\langle|I|^{-1}\rangle^{-c}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big\|_{X_{N}^{*}(I)}

and the proof of (
3.1
) and (
3.1
) follows.

It remains to prove (
3
). In the following, we prove appropriate inequalities for every summand in the definition of
\widetilde{X}_{N}
. Since the summands of
\widetilde{Y}_{N}
are the same, the claimed bound (
3
) also follows for
\widetilde{X}_{N}
replaced by
\widetilde{Y}_{N}
.

Let us prove a more general statement for functions
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
that have disjoint support in time. Since
(a+b)^{s}\leq a^{s}+b^{s}
for any
s\in[0,1]
, we can use the triangle inequality to deduce that for any
1\leq r\leq p\leq\infty
we have

\big\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{p}L_{x}^{q}(I)}^{r}\begin{aligned} &\leq\Big\|\|v_{1}\|_{L_{x}^{q}}+\|v_{2}\|_{L_{x}^{q}}\Big\|_{L_{t}^{p}(I)}^{r}\\
&=\Big(\int_{I}\big(\|v_{1}(t)\|^{p}_{L^{q}_{x}}+\|v_{2}(t)\|^{p}_{L^{q}_{x}}\big)\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\Big)^{\frac{r}{p}}\\
&\leq\Big(\int_{I}\|v_{1}(t)\|^{p}_{L^{q}_{x}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\Big)^{\frac{r}{p}}+\Big(\int_{I}\|v_{2}(t)\|^{p}_{L^{q}_{x}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\Big)^{\frac{r}{p}}\\
&=\|v_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{p}L_{x}^{q}(I)}^{r}+\|v_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{p}L_{x}^{q}(I)}^{r}.\end{aligned}

For the directional norms, fix
\mathfrak{c}\in[1,\infty)

and assume
1\leq r\leq\min\{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}\}
. Let us show that for
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
with disjoint supports (in time) we have

\displaystyle\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{r}\leq\|v_{1}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{r}+\|v_{2}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{r}.

Indeed for any fixed
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
, that

\displaystyle\Big\|\|v_{1}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})+v_{2}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\Big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\|v_{1}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}+\|v_{2}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\|v_{1}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\Big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}+\Big\|\|v_{2}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\Big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}.

First, we assume that
\mathfrak{a}\leq\mathfrak{b}
and in particular
r\leq\mathfrak{a}
. Using that
(a+b)^{s}\leq a^{s}+b^{s}
for any
s\in[0,1]
we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\|v_{1}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})+v_{2}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\Big\|^{\mathfrak{a}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\big\|\|v_{1}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}+\big\|\|v_{2}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}\Big)^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\|v_{1}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\big\|^{\mathfrak{a}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}+\big\|\|v_{2}(t,x_{1},x^{\prime})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\big\|^{\mathfrak{a}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}(I)}.

Integrating in
x_{1}
yields

\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\leq\|v_{1}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{\mathfrak{a}}+\|v_{2}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\,.

After raising both sides to the power
\frac{r}{\mathfrak{a}}
and using again that
(a+b)^{s}\leq a^{s}+b^{s}
, for
s=\frac{r}{\mathfrak{a}}\leq 1
, it follows

\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{r}\leq\|v_{1}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{r}+\|v_{2}\|_{L^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}_{e_{1}}}^{r},

as desired.

Second, we assume that
\mathfrak{a}\geq\mathfrak{b}
, and in particular that
r\leq\mathfrak{b}
. Then, (
3
) and the triangle inequality yield

\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|_{L_{e_{1}}^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\begin{aligned} &\leq\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\big\|\|v_{1}(x_{1})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}}\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}}+\big\|\|v_{2}(x_{1})\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}}\big)^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x_{1}\Big)^{\frac{r}{\mathfrak{a}}}\\
&=\Big\|\big\|\|v_{1}\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}}+\big\|\|v_{2}\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}}\Big\|_{L^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}}_{x_{1}}}^{\frac{r}{\mathfrak{b}}}\\
&\leq\Big(\Big\|\big\|\|v_{1}\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}}\Big\|_{L^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}}_{x_{1}}}+\Big\|\big\|\|v_{2}\|_{L^{\mathfrak{c}}_{x^{\prime}}}\big\|^{\mathfrak{b}}_{L^{\mathfrak{b}}_{t}}\Big\|_{L^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}}_{x_{1}}}\Big)^{\frac{r}{\mathfrak{b}}}\\
&=\Big(\|v_{1}\|_{L_{e_{1}}^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\mathfrak{b}}+\|v_{2}\|_{L_{e_{1}}^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\mathfrak{b}}\Big)^{\frac{r}{\mathfrak{b}}}\\
&\leq\|v_{1}\|_{L_{e_{1}}^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}+\|v_{2}\|_{L_{e_{1}}^{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c})}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r},\end{aligned}

as desired.

Having shown that (
3
) holds, the desired bound (
3
) follows for
r=\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}
, which indeed satisfies
r\leq\min\{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}\}

for any directional norm in the definition of
X_{N}(I)
or
Y_{N}(I)
.
The proof of the desired results is finished.
∎

4.
The multilinear estimates

We start this section by proving a bilinear estimates in terms of
directional space-time norms. Then, we derive crucial trilinear
estimates that allow us to control the cubic nonlinearity in
(
1
).

Lemma 4.1
.

Fix any
0<\varepsilon\lesssim 1
and
0<\varepsilon_{0}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}1
. For any two functions

h_{+},h_{-}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
and any
N_{+},N_{-}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
with
N_{+}\geq N_{-}

it holds that

\displaystyle\|h_{+}h_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon_{0}}N_{+}^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{N_{+}}{N_{-}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\times\begin{cases}N_{-}^{\mathfrak{s}_{c}}\|h_{+}\|_{X_{N_{+}}(\mathbb{R})}\|h_{-}\|_{X_{N_{-}}(\mathbb{R})},\\
\|h_{+}\|_{Y_{N_{+}}(\mathbb{R})}\|h_{-}\|_{Y_{N_{-}}(\mathbb{R})},\\
\|h_{+}\|_{X_{N_{+}}(\mathbb{R})}\|h_{-}\|_{Y_{N_{-}}(\mathbb{R})},\\
\|h_{+}\|_{Y_{N_{+}}(\mathbb{R})}\|h_{-}\|_{X_{N_{-}}(\mathbb{R})}\,,\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{s}_{c}\coloneqq\frac{d-2}{2}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}
be as in
Lemma

2.5
, and recall that

\sum_{l}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}=\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{Id}}\mathopen{}
. The triangle inequality implies

\|h_{+}\,h_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\sum_{l=1}^{d}\big\|(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+})\,h_{-}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,,

and therefore it suffices to prove (
4.1
)
for
h_{+}
replaced by
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}
.

By Fubini’s Theorem and Hölder’s inequality we obtain that

\big\|(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+})\,h_{-}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\begin{aligned} &=\big\|(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+})\,h_{-}\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(2,2,2)}(\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(\mathbb{R})}\|h_{-}\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(\mathbb{R})}\,.\end{aligned}

Then, the first three bounds in (
4.1
) follow
from the definitions (
3
) and (
3
) of the norms
X_{N}
and
Y_{N}
.

To prove the last estimate in (
4.1
), we use Hölder’s inequality to obtain that

\big\|(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+})\,h_{-}\big\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(2,2,2)}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{L_{e_{1}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{\mathfrak{c}_{0}-2(1-\varepsilon_{0})})}(\mathbb{R})}\|h_{-}\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(\mathbb{R})}\,.

Then from the definition (
3
) of the norm
X_{N}
it follows that

\|h_{-}\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim N_{-}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|h_{-}\|_{X_{N_{-}}(\mathbb{R})}\,.

In addition, since
\mathfrak{c}_{0}=2\frac{d-1}{d-2}\geq 2
, it holds that

\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}}\leq\frac{2\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{\mathfrak{c}_{0}-2(1-\varepsilon_{0})}\leq\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}}

for any
0<\varepsilon_{0}<2^{-100}
. Thus, interpolation with an appropriately chosen parameter
\theta\in[0,1]
and the definition (
3
) of

Y_{N}
, give that

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2\mathfrak{c}_{0}}{\mathfrak{c}_{0}-2(1-\varepsilon_{0})})}(\mathbb{R})}\hskip-6.99997pt\leq\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}})}(\mathbb{R})}^{1-\theta}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{L_{e_{l}}^{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{0}})}(\mathbb{R})}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{+}^{\frac{1-\theta}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{Y_{N_{+}}(\mathbb{R})}^{1-\theta}N_{+}^{\frac{\theta}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{Y_{N_{+}}(\mathbb{R})}^{\theta}=N_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{U}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{e_{l}}h_{+}\|_{Y_{N_{+}}(\mathbb{R})}

and the proof is finished.
∎

The above lemma is a crucial ingredient for showing that product of
three functions taken from the spaces
Y^{S}
or
X^{\mathfrak{s}}
lies in the
space
X^{*,\sigma_{*}}
, which we use to control the non-homogeneity

h
in (
3
). The specific
regularity of the product depends on whether the functions in play
belong to
Y^{S}
or to
X^{\mathfrak{s}}
and on the respective regularity
exponents
S
and
\mathfrak{s}
. Since the three-fold product of functions is
estimated in the norm
X^{*,\sigma_{*}}
, defined by duality in
(
3
), the proof of

Proposition

4.2
naturally reduces to showing bounds on
a
4
-linear integral form, formulated in
Lemma

4.3
. The

4
-linear estimate (
4.3
) of
Lemma

4.3

in turn can be reduced using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the
bilinear
L^{2}
estimate of
Lemma

4.1
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Fix any

0<S_{1}\leq S_{2}\leq S_{3}<\mathfrak{s}_{c}<\mathfrak{s}\,,

and choose any
0<\varepsilon\lesssim_{S_{j},\mathfrak{s}}\!1
and

0<\varepsilon_{0}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,S_{j},\mathfrak{s}}\!1
. Then for any

z_{j}
and
v_{j}
,
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\big\|z_{1}z_{2}z_{3}\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma_{*}}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|z_{1}\|_{Y^{S_{1}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|z_{2}\|_{Y^{S_{2}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|z_{3}\|_{Y^{S_{3}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\text{with }\sigma_{*}\coloneqq S_{1}+\min\Big(S_{2},\frac{1}{2}\Big)+\min\Big(S_{3},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\,.

\displaystyle\big\|z_{1}z_{2}v_{3}\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma_{*}}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|z_{1}\|_{Y^{S_{1}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|z_{2}\|_{Y^{S_{2}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|v_{3}\|_{X^{\mathfrak{s}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\text{with }\sigma_{*}\coloneqq S_{1}+\min\Big(S_{2},\frac{1}{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\,.

\displaystyle\big\|z_{1}v_{2}v_{3}\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma_{*}}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|z_{1}\|_{Y^{S_{1}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|v_{2}\|_{X^{\mathfrak{s}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|v_{3}\|_{X^{\mathfrak{s}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\text{with }\sigma_{*}\coloneqq\min\big(S_{1}+\mathfrak{s},\,S_{1}+1\big)\,.

\displaystyle\big\|v_{1}v_{2}v_{3}\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma_{*}}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|v_{1}\|_{X^{\mathfrak{s}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|v_{2}\|_{X^{\mathfrak{s}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}\|v_{3}\|_{X^{\mathfrak{s}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\text{with }\sigma_{*}\coloneqq\mathfrak{s}+2(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}_{c})\,.

The implicit constants are allowed to depend on
\varepsilon
,
\varepsilon_{0}
,
\mathfrak{s}
, and
S_{j}
.

We deduce
Proposition

4.2
by duality from
Lemma

4.3
below, which is proved at the end of the section.

Before proceeding, we introduce some notation. For
\sigma_{*}
as in each
case of
Proposition

4.2
(detailed below) we choose

v_{*}\in X^{-\sigma_{*}}(\mathbb{R})
. Next, for any function

h\in\{v_{*},z_{j},v_{j}\}
,
j=1,2,3
, and
N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
we define
quantities

Z_{N}(h)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\|v_{*}\|_{X_{N}(\mathbb{R})}&\text{if }h=v_{*}\,,\\
\|v_{j}\|_{X_{N}(\mathbb{R})}&\text{if }h=v_{j}\,,\\
\|z_{j}\|_{Y_{N}(\mathbb{R})}&\text{if }h=z_{j}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\alpha(h)\coloneqq\begin{cases}-\sigma_{*}&\text{if }h=v_{*}\,,\\
\mathfrak{s}&\text{if }h=v_{j}\,,\\
S_{j}&\text{if }h=z_{j}\,,\end{cases}

and we denote by
\mathscr{S}(h_{1},\cdots,h_{4})
the set of permutations of a quadruple
(h_{1},\cdots,h_{4})
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Fix exponents as in (
4.2
), and
choose any
0<\varepsilon\lesssim 1
and

0<\varepsilon_{0}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,S_{j},\mathfrak{s}}1
. Then for any

N_{j}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
,
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{4}}h_{4}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x=0

unless
N_{j}\lesssim\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}N_{j^{\prime}}
for all
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. Furthermore,

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{4}}h_{4}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|\lesssim(N_{1}N_{2}N_{3}N_{4})^{\varepsilon}\prod_{j=1}^{4}N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})}Z_{N_{j}}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j}\big)

provided that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

Case
zzz
:

(h_{1},\cdots,h_{4})\in\mathscr{S}(v_{*},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})
and
\sigma_{*}
as in (
4.2
).

Case
zzv
:

(h_{1},\cdots,h_{4})\in\mathscr{S}(v_{*},z_{1},z_{2},v_{3})
and
\sigma_{*}
as in (
4.2
).

Case
zvv
:

(h_{1},\cdots,h_{4})\in\mathscr{S}(v_{*},z_{1},v_{2},v_{3})
and
\sigma_{*}
as in (
4.2
).

Case
vvv
:

(h_{1},\cdots,h_{4})\in\mathscr{S}(v_{*},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})
and
\sigma_{*}
as in (
4.2
).

Proof of
Proposition

4.2
.

Choose any
h_{j}\in\{z_{j},v_{j}\}
,
j=\{1,2,3\}
. The definition of
X^{*,\sigma_{*}}
and
X_{N}
in (
3
),
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{Id}}\mathopen{}=\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}
, the triangle inequality, and
\Big(\sum|a_{j}|^{2}\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq\sum|a_{j}|
imply that

\big\|h_{1}h_{2}h_{3}\big\|_{X^{*,\sigma_{*}}(\mathbb{R})}\begin{aligned} &=\Big(\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{2\sigma_{*}}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\big(h_{1}h_{2}h_{3}\big)\big\|_{X^{*}_{N}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
&\leq\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{\sigma_{*}}\big\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}\big(P_{N_{1}}h_{1}P_{N_{2}}h_{2}P_{N_{3}}h_{3}\big)\big\|_{X^{*}_{N}(\mathbb{R})}\\
&\leq\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{\sigma_{*}}\sup_{v_{*}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v_{*}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|\,,\end{aligned}

where the upper bound in the last expression is taken over all
v_{*}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v_{*}\|_{X_{N}}\leq 1
. Note that the
assumptions of
Lemma

4.3
on
S_{j}
,
\mathfrak{s}
,
\varepsilon
,
\varepsilon_{0}
, and
\sigma_{*}

coincide with those of
Proposition

4.2
, and therefore (
4.3
)
with
\varepsilon
replaced by
\frac{\varepsilon}{8}
yields

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}v_{*}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|\begin{aligned} &\lesssim(N_{1}N_{2}N_{3}N)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}\prod_{j=1}^{3}N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})}Z_{N_{j}}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j}\big)\\
&\hskip-20.00003pt=(N_{1}N_{2}N_{3})^{-\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}}N^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}\prod_{j=1}^{3}N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})+\varepsilon/2}Z_{N_{j}}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j}\big)\,.\end{aligned}

We may assume that
N\lesssim\max_{j}N_{j}
since otherwise, by (
4.3
), the left-hand side vanishes. We have that
(N_{1}N_{2}N_{3})^{-\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}}N^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}\lesssim N^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}
, and consequently

\displaystyle\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}(N_{1}N_{2}N_{3})^{-\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}}N^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}\prod_{j=1}^{3}N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})+\varepsilon}Z_{N_{j}}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}\prod_{j=1}^{3}\Big(\sum_{N_{j}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})+\varepsilon/2}Z_{N_{j}}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j}\big)\Big)\,.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the definitions of
Z_{N_{j}}(h_{j})
,
\alpha(h_{j})
,
X^{\mathfrak{s}}
, and
Y^{S}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{N_{j}\in 2^{N}}N^{\alpha(h_{j})+\varepsilon/2}Z_{N_{j}}(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j})

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}N_{j}^{-\varepsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{N\in 2^{N}}N_{j}^{2\alpha(h)+2\varepsilon}Z_{N_{j}}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j}\big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\begin{cases}\|z_{j}\|_{Y^{S_{j}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}&\text{if }h=z_{j}\,,\\
\|v_{j}\|_{X^{\mathfrak{s}+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})}&\text{if }h=v_{j}\,.\end{cases}

∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.3
.

Note that the right-hand sides of (
4.3
) and (
4.3
), as well as the subsequent conditions are the same if we exchange
P_{N_{j}}h_{j}
with
P_{N_{j^{\prime}}}h_{j^{\prime}}

j,j^{\prime}\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, and therefore we assume without loss of generality, that
N_{1}\geq N_{2}\geq N_{3}\geq N_{4}
.

First, we prove an orthogonality observation
(
4.3
). For a contradiction suppose that

N_{1}\lesssim N_{2}+N_{3}+N_{4}
does not hold, and in particular, suppose
that
N_{1}\geq 2^{5}N_{2}
. By (
2
) we have

\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathcal{F}}\mathopen{}(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1})\big)\subset B_{N_{1}}\setminus B_{2^{-2}N_{1}}
, since

N_{1}\geq 2^{5}N_{2}\geq 2^{5}>1
. On the other hand, since
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathcal{F}}\mathopen{}(h_{2}h_{3}h_{4})=\widehat{h_{2}}*\widehat{h_{3}}*\widehat{h_{4}}
, it holds that

\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathcal{F}}\mathopen{}(h_{2}h_{3}h_{4})\big)\subset\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}\big(\widehat{h_{2}}\big)+\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}\big(\widehat{h_{3}}\big)+\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}\big(\widehat{h_{4}}\big),

and since
N_{2}\geq N_{3}\geq N_{4}
,
N_{1}\geq 2^{5}N_{2}
, and
\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}\Big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathcal{F}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N}h\big)\Big)\subset B_{N}
by (
2
), we have

\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{spt}}\mathopen{}\Big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathcal{F}}\mathopen{}\big(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{4}}h_{4}\big)\Big)\subset B_{N_{2}}+B_{N_{3}}+B_{N_{4}}\subset B_{2^{3}N_{2}}\subset B_{2^{-2}N_{1}}\,,

Thus (
4.3
) holds by Plancherel’s identity.

We henceforth suppose that
N_{1}\approx N_{2}
and we proceed to prove bound (
4.3
).
To estimate the left-hand side of (
4.3
) we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality pairing one function with high frequency (
N_{1}
or
N_{2}
) with a function with a lower frequency (
N_{3}
or
N_{4}
). This gives the bound

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{4}}h_{4}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\min\Big(\begin{aligned} &\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{4}}h_{4}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},\\
&\qquad\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{4}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big).\end{aligned}

By definitions in (
4
) and
Lemma

4.1
for any
l<l^{\prime}\in\{1,2,3,4\}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{l}}h_{l}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{l^{\prime}}}h_{l^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{l}^{\varepsilon-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{l})}N_{l^{\prime}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{l^{\prime}})+\beta(h_{l},h_{l^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j\in\{l,l^{\prime}\}}N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})}Z_{N_{j}}(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j})\,,

where

\beta(h_{l},h_{l^{\prime}})\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathfrak{s}_{c}&\text{if }h_{l},h_{l^{\prime}}\in\{v_{*},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\}\,,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Hence, since
N_{1}\approx N_{2}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{1}}h_{1}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{2}}h_{2}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{3}}h_{3}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{4}}h_{4}\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}t\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{d}}\mathopen{}x\Big|\leq N_{1}^{2\varepsilon-1-\alpha(h_{1})-\alpha(h_{2})}N_{3}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{3})}N_{4}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{3})}

\displaystyle\times\min\Big(N_{3}^{\beta(h_{2},h_{3})}N_{4}^{\beta(h_{1},h_{4})},N_{3}^{\beta(h_{1},h_{3})}N_{4}^{\beta(h_{2},h_{4})}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{4}N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})}Z_{N_{j}}(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j})\,.

By homogeneity of the required bound (
4.3
) we can assume, without loss of generality, that
N_{j}^{\alpha(h_{j})}Z_{N_{j}}(\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathrm{P}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathopen{}_{N_{j}}h_{j})=1
,
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. Thus, recalling that
N_{1}\approx N_{2}
, we reduce (
4.3
) to proving

(4.12)

N_{3}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{3})-\varepsilon}N_{4}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{4})-\varepsilon}\min\Big(N_{3}^{\beta(h_{2},h_{3})}N_{4}^{\beta(h_{1},h_{4})},N_{3}^{\beta(h_{1},h_{3})}N_{4}^{\beta(h_{2},h_{4})}\Big)\\
\lesssim N_{1}^{1+\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})}

We first claim that
\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+1\geq 0
. Indeed,
\alpha(h)<0
only if
h=v_{*}
, in which case
\alpha(h)=-\sigma_{*}
. Assuming, without loss of generality, that
h_{1}=v_{*}
, in each of
the cases zzz, zzv, or zvv, we replace the minimum in (
4.2
), in (
4.2
), or in
(
4.2
) by
\frac{1}{2}
or by
S+1
to obtain that

\sigma_{*}\leq S_{1}+1\leq S_{j}+1\leq\mathfrak{s}+1,\qquad j\in\{1,2,3\};

the claim follows. Finally, in the case vvv we have
h_{2}=v
, and therefore
\alpha(h_{2})=\mathfrak{s}
. Consequently,

\sigma_{*}=\mathfrak{s}+2(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}_{c})\leq\mathfrak{s}+1,

and the claim follows.

Thus, since
N_{1}\geq N_{3}
it is sufficient to show (
4.12
) for
N_{1}=N_{3}
, that is, to show

\displaystyle\min\Big(N_{3}^{\beta(h_{2},h_{3})}N_{4}^{\beta(h_{1},h_{4})},N_{3}^{\beta(h_{1},h_{3})}N_{4}^{\beta(h_{2},h_{4})}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{3}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})}N_{4}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\alpha(h_{4})}

After standard algebraic manipulations, (
4
) follows if we show that for any
N_{3}\geq N_{4}
either

N_{4}^{\beta(h_{1},h_{4})+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{4})}\lesssim N_{3}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})-\beta(h_{2},h_{3})}

or

N_{4}^{\beta(h_{2},h_{4})+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{4})}\lesssim N_{3}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})-\beta(h_{1},h_{3})}\,.

Since
N_{3}\geq N_{4}
are arbitrary, it suffices to
show that either

\max\{0,\beta(h_{1},h_{4})+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{4})\}\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})-\beta(h_{2},h_{3})

or

\max\{0,\beta(h_{2},h_{4})+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha(h_{4})\}\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})-\beta(h_{1},h_{3})

holds. The expressions above can be rewritten in more symmetric form as

\beta(h_{2},h_{3})+\max\{\alpha(h_{4})-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\beta(h_{1},h_{4})\}\leq\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})+\alpha(h_{4})

or

\beta(h_{1},h_{3})+\max\{\alpha(h_{4})-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\beta(h_{2},h_{4})\}\leq\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})+\alpha(h_{4})

Next, we discuss each case separately.

Case
zzz
. Then,

\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})+\alpha(h_{4})=-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}

and
\beta(h_{j},h_{k})=0
for any
j\neq k
, and therefore (
4
) is equivalent to

\max\{\alpha(h_{4})-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},0\}\leq-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}\,.

Clearly the left hand side is largest if

\alpha(h_{4})=S_{3}
, and consequently (
4
) holds if

\sigma_{*}\leq S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}-\max\{S_{3}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},0\}=S_{1}+S_{2}+\min\{S_{3},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\}\,,

which holds by (
4.2
).

Case
zzv
. Then,

\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})+\alpha(h_{4})=-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+S_{2}+\mathfrak{s}

and either
\beta(h_{2},h_{3})=0
or
\beta(h_{1},h_{3})=0
. Suppose
\beta(h_{2},h_{3})=0
, the case
\beta(h_{1},h_{3})=0

follows analogously by proving (
4
) instead of (
4
). Then, (
4
)
is equivalent to

\max\{\alpha(h_{4})-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\beta(h_{1},h_{4})\}\leq-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+S_{2}+\mathfrak{s}\,.

Since
\alpha(h_{4})\leq\mathfrak{s}
and
\beta(h_{1},h_{4})\leq\mathfrak{s}_{c}
, then (
4
) follows if we show that

\max\{\mathfrak{s}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\mathfrak{s}_{c}\}\leq-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+S_{2}+\mathfrak{s}\,,

which is equivalent to

\sigma_{*}\leq S_{1}+S_{2}+\mathfrak{s}+\min\{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}_{c}\}\,,

that follows from (
4.2
).

Case
zvv
. Then,

\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})+\alpha(h_{4})=-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+\mathfrak{s}+\mathfrak{s}\,.

We discuss three cases. If
h_{4}=v
, then
z_{1}\in\{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\}
and consequently either

\beta(h_{2},h_{3})=0
or
\beta(h_{1},h_{3})=0
. Suppose
\beta(h_{2},h_{3})=0
, the case
\beta(h_{1},h_{3})=0

follows analogously by proving (
4
) instead of (
4
). Then, (
4
)
is equivalent to

\max\{\mathfrak{s}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\beta(h_{1},h_{4})\}\leq-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+2\mathfrak{s}\,.

Since
\beta(h_{1},h_{4})\leq\mathfrak{s}_{c}
, then (
4
) follows if we show that

\max\{\mathfrak{s}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\mathfrak{s}_{c}\}\leq-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+2\mathfrak{s}\,,

which is equivalent to

\sigma_{*}\leq S_{1}+\mathfrak{s}+\min\{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}_{c}\}\,,

that follows from (
4.2
).

If
h_{4}=z_{1}
, then
\beta(h_{1},h_{4})=0
,

\beta(h_{2},h_{3})\leq\mathfrak{s}_{c}
,
and
\alpha(h_{4})=S_{1}
implies that
(
4
) holds if

\mathfrak{s}_{c}+\max\{S_{1}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},0\}\leq-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+2\mathfrak{s}\,.

After standard algebraic manipulations,
the latter is equivalent to

\sigma_{*}\leq 2\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}_{c}+\min\{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},S_{1}\}\,,

which follows from (
4.2
).

If
h_{4}=v_{*}
, then
\alpha(h_{4})-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}<0
, and either
\beta(h_{2},h_{3})=0
or
\beta(h_{1},h_{3})=0
. As above, we suppose
\beta(h_{2},h_{3})=0

and we note that (
4
) follows if we show

\mathfrak{s}_{c}\leq-\sigma_{*}+S_{1}+2\mathfrak{s},

or equivalently

\sigma_{*}\leq S_{1}+2\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}_{c},

which follows from (
4.2
).

Case
vvv
. Then,

\alpha(h_{1})+\alpha(h_{2})+\alpha(h_{3})+\alpha(h_{4})=-\sigma_{*}+3\mathfrak{s}

and
\beta(h_{j},h_{k})\leq\mathfrak{s}_{c}
for each
j\neq k
. Then, (
4
) follows if we show

\mathfrak{s}_{c}+\max\{\mathfrak{s}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\mathfrak{s}_{c}\}\leq-\sigma_{*}+3\mathfrak{s}\,.

The latter is equivalent to

\sigma_{*}\leq 2(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}_{c})+\min\{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\mathfrak{s}_{c},\mathfrak{s}\}\,,

and (
4
) follows from (
4.2
).
∎

We conclude this section by a crucial estimate that justifies the regularity computation encoded by the definition (
1.3
) of
\mu(k,S)

Lemma 4.4

(Inductive property of
\mu(k,S)
)
.

For any
S>0
and any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
, with

k_{1}\leq k_{2}\leq k_{3}
, it holds that

\mu(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},S)\leq\mu(k_{1},S)+\min\big(\mu(k_{2},S),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\big)+\min\big(\mu(k_{3},S),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\big).

Furthermore, setting
k\coloneqq k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}

0<\varepsilon\lesssim_{k}1
, and any

0<\varepsilon_{0}\lesssim_{k,S,\varepsilon}1
. Then for any functions

z_{j}
,
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, it holds that

\|z_{1}z_{2}z_{3}\|_{X^{*,\mu(k,S)}}\lesssim\prod_{j=1}^{3}\|z_{j}\|_{Y^{\mu(k_{j},S)+\varepsilon}}\,,
